Surah: 82. Follow the brief below (surah_map.md) exactly. The evidence is text.md (the surah), dictionary.md (every root of the surah's words, each branch with the classical dictionaries' own phrases), channels.md and hft.md (both are earlier readers' proposals: ignore their judgements (grades, strength or confidence labels, reading types, statements of what a reading may or may not do) and make your own) and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Return only the map as your final message.

===== _commentary/v16/prompts/r2/surah_map.md =====
Read the surah as one text and write its map of image chains: the lexical images that run through several of
its ayat and join them into one scene, process or movement. A later writer will read one ayah at a time, with
only that ayah's own dictionary. The map lets that writer hear what the ayah's words carry in the surah as a
whole, including senses whose evidence sits under the words of other ayat.

Your evidence is the surah text, the dictionary of every root in the surah (each branch with the classical
dictionaries' own phrases), earlier readers' channel review (channels.md) and activation hypotheses (hft.md),
and your own knowledge of Arabic and the Quran. Where a member or passage comes from memory rather than from
the dictionary or the text, say so. Do not use tools, delegate, browse or inspect files.

The channel review and the HFT records are proposals by earlier readers. Ignore their judgements: grades,
strength or confidence labels, reading types, words such as "surprising", "exploratory" or "latent", and every
statement of what a reading may or may not do. Make your own judgement from the surah's words, the dictionary
phrases and the Quran. Do not rediscover what they already assembled: start from their chains, test each one
against the dictionary phrases and the text, and join, extend, split or correct them. Where their wording
abstracts a member, go back to the dictionary phrase and name what it actually says.

A chain belongs on the map when its members are senses the dictionary attests for words that stand in the
surah, and together they make one image or process that the surah's wording or sequence lets a listener hear.
A member need not be the sense that translates its word in its own ayah; a chain is heard across the surah,
not in one word. Say plainly when a member is a rare sense or is attested only inside a fixed expression, and
keep it. When the dictionary itself joins two of the surah's words in one phrase, quote that phrase: it is
the strongest evidence a chain can have. Keep a scene at the level of its objects, their parts and their
operation. When proposals share members, do not fold one into another's more abstract function unless nothing
concrete is lost. Carry every chain that meets this test, however unusual; leave out proposals that do not.

Write the map in English, with Arabic quoted exactly (surah wording from the text, dictionary phrases from the
dictionary). It is working material for the writer, not commentary prose.

1. `## Chains`. For each chain, a `###` heading naming the image. One paragraph on what the image is and how it
   moves through the surah. Then its members, one line each: the ayah ref, the word as it stands in the text,
   the root and branch id, the dictionary's own phrase quoted exactly, and what this member contributes to the
   image. Add Quran passages outside the surah, with exact refs, where they stage or confirm the chain.
2. `## Ayat`. For each ayah in order: which chains it opens, advances or completes, and what its words add to
   each.
3. `## Not carried`. Each channel subchannel and each HFT record you did not carry into a chain, one line each:
   its name and why.

No ranking and no labels of strength or confidence. No list of what a writer must include. There is no length
target and no required number of chains or members.


===== _commentary/v16/work/s082/surah.r2/text.md =====
# Surah 82

- 82:1 إِذَا ٱلسَّمَآءُ ٱنفَطَرَتْ
- 82:2 وَإِذَا ٱلْكَوَاكِبُ ٱنتَثَرَتْ
- 82:3 وَإِذَا ٱلْبِحَارُ فُجِّرَتْ
- 82:4 وَإِذَا ٱلْقُبُورُ بُعْثِرَتْ
- 82:5 عَلِمَتْ نَفْسٌۭ مَّا قَدَّمَتْ وَأَخَّرَتْ
- 82:6 يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلْإِنسَٰنُ مَا غَرَّكَ بِرَبِّكَ ٱلْكَرِيمِ
- 82:7 ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَكَ فَسَوَّىٰكَ فَعَدَلَكَ
- 82:8 فِىٓ أَىِّ صُورَةٍۢ مَّا شَآءَ رَكَّبَكَ
- 82:9 كَلَّا بَلْ تُكَذِّبُونَ بِٱلدِّينِ
- 82:10 وَإِنَّ عَلَيْكُمْ لَحَٰفِظِينَ
- 82:11 كِرَامًۭا كَٰتِبِينَ
- 82:12 يَعْلَمُونَ مَا تَفْعَلُونَ
- 82:13 إِنَّ ٱلْأَبْرَارَ لَفِى نَعِيمٍۢ
- 82:14 وَإِنَّ ٱلْفُجَّارَ لَفِى جَحِيمٍۢ
- 82:15 يَصْلَوْنَهَا يَوْمَ ٱلدِّينِ
- 82:16 وَمَا هُمْ عَنْهَا بِغَآئِبِينَ
- 82:17 وَمَآ أَدْرَىٰكَ مَا يَوْمُ ٱلدِّينِ
- 82:18 ثُمَّ مَآ أَدْرَىٰكَ مَا يَوْمُ ٱلدِّينِ
- 82:19 يَوْمَ لَا تَمْلِكُ نَفْسٌۭ لِّنَفْسٍۢ شَيْـًۭٔا ۖ وَٱلْأَمْرُ يَوْمَئِذٍۢ لِّلَّهِ


===== _commentary/v16/work/s082/surah.r2/dictionary.md =====
# Dictionary: every branch of every focus root

Every root of the surah's words, each once, headed by the ayah and word where it first occurs; it also serves the later words listed with it.
One entry per branch: a Turkish label and Turkish glosses of its attested senses, then the classical dictionaries' own phrases with their source tags (ayn, sihah, tahdhib, maqayis, mufradat, jamhara). [kalıp] marks a sense the dictionaries attest only inside a fixed expression.
Identity roots come from the quran-data gateway. Word-scoped alternatives are cited analyses of this
exact word. **Echo roots** are observed but withheld mappings: sound-family candidates, not identity.

## ق د م (root_001207): 82:5 قَدَّمَتْ

- **B001** ayak — ayak

- **B004** öne geçme, önde ilerleme veya erken davranma — öne geçmek; önde olmak · öne geçme ve ilerleme · ön taraf; önde · önüne geçmek; vaktinden önce davranmak · duraksamadan ileri gitmek
  قدم فلان قومه أي يكون أمامهم والقدم المضي أمام ويمضي قدما أي لا ينثني وقدام خلاف وراء والقدم ضد الأخر (ayn)؛ قدم أي تقدم وقدم بين يديه أي تقدم ومضى قدما لم يعرج ولم ينثن واستقدم وتقدم بمعنى ومقدم نقيض مؤخر (sihah)؛ القدم المضي وهو الإقدام وقدام خلاف وراء والقدم ضد أخر وقدم فلان فلانا إذا تقدمه ولا تقدموا معناه لا تتقدموا قبل الوقت (tahdhib)؛ به اعتبر التقدم والتأخر (mufradat)؛ أصل صحيح يدل على سبق وأصله مضى فلان قدما لم يعرج ولم ينثن ومقدمة الجيش أوله (maqayis)
- **B005** yolculuktan dönüş — yolculuktan dönmek; geri gelmek · yolculuktan dönüş; yolcunun geliş zamanı · yolculuktan dönenler
  القدوم الرجوع من السفر وقدم يقدم (ayn)؛ قدم من سفره قدوما ومقدما والقدام القادمون من سفر (sihah)؛ القدوم الإياب من السفر وقدم فلان من سفره يقدم قدوما (tahdhib)؛ من الباب قدم من سفره قدوما ويقال القدام القادمون من سفر (maqayis)
- **B006** cesaretle öne atılma ve gözüpeklik — gözüpeklik; cesaretle öne atılma · bir işe cesaretle girişmek · gözüpek, atılgan savaşçı · ileri! · gözüpek ve öne atılan kişi
  رجل قدم مقتحم للأشياء يتقدم الناس ويمضي في الحرب قدما (ayn)؛ أقدم على الأمر إقداما والإقدام الشجاعة وأقدم زجر للفرس والمقدام الكثير الإقدام على العدو (sihah)؛ أقدم على قرنه إقداما وقدما ومقدما إذا تقدم عليه بجرأة وضده الإحجام ورجل مقدام في الحرب جريء (tahdhib)؛ أقدم على الشيء إقداما وأقدم زجر للفرس ومضى القوم في الحرب اليقدمية (maqayis)
- **B007** ön bölüm veya ilk kesim — ordunun öncü birliği · gözün veya başın ön kısmı · alın önü ve perçem bölgesi · kuşun ön kanat telekleri · eyer takımının ön kısmı · deve veya ineğin öndeki iki meme başı · dağın öne çıkan burnu · yüzüstü düşmek
  مقدم العين ما يلي الأنف والمقدمة الناصية وما استقبلك من الجبهة والجبين وقادمة الرحل والقادمة الريشة التي تلي منكب الجناح (ayn)؛ قوادم الطير مقاديم ريشه وقيدوم الجبل أنف يتقدم منه وقيدوم كل شيء مقدمه وصدره ومقدمة الجيش أوله وقادمتا الناقة (sihah)؛ مقدمة الجيش الذين يتقدمون الجيش ومقدم العين ومقدم الرأس والمقدمة الناصية وقادمة الرحل وللناقة قادمان وقوادم ريش الطائر (tahdhib)؛ قادمة الرحل خلاف آخرته وقادمة من أطباء الناقة وقادم الإنسان رأسه وقوادم الطير ومقدمة الجيش أوله وقيدوم الجبل أنف يتقدم منه (maqayis)
- **B008** ahşap yontma keseri — ahşap yontma keseri
  القدوم مخفقة الحديدة التي ينحت بها الخشب (ayn)؛ القدوم التي ينحت بها مخففة ولا تقل قدوم بالتشديد (sihah)؛ القدوم التي ينحت بها وجمعها قدم واختتن إبراهيم بالقدوم قال قطعه بها (tahdhib)؛ مما شذ عن هذا الأصل القدوم الحديدة ينحت بها وهي معروفة (maqayis)
- **B009** belirli bir yer adı — belirli bir yer adı

- **B005** belirti veya belirlenmiş vakit — belirti, belirlenmiş zaman veya buluşma vakti · yolun işaretleri · çöl veya yol üzerindeki küçük işaret taşı
  الأمارة الموعد، والأمارة العلامة، والأمار أمار الطريق معالمه (maqayis)؛ الأمار الموعد (ayn)؛ الأمار والأمارة الوقت والعلامة، والأمر بالتحريك جمع أمرة وهي العلم الصغير من أعلام المفاوز من الحجارة (sihah)؛ الأمار الوقت والعلامة، والأمرات الأعلام واحدتها أمرة (tahdhib)
- **B006** ağır ve yadırganan şey — büyük, ağır, yadırganan veya şaşırtıcı iş · büyük ve yadırganan bir şey
  العجب، لقد جئت شيئا إمرا (maqayis)؛ أمر أمره أي اشتد والاسم الإمر، ويقال عجبا (sihah)؛ لقد جئت شيئا إمرا أي جئت شيئا عظيما من المنكر (tahdhib)؛ إمرا أي منكرا، من قولهم أمر الأمر أي كبر وكثر (mufradat)
- **B007** danışıp görüş oluşturma — işimde ona danıştım · karşılıklı danışma veya birbirinin görüşünü kabul etme · kendi içinde düşünüp görüşünü karara bağladı · senin hakkında birbirleriyle danışıyorlar
  فلان يؤامر نفسيه أي نفس تأمره بشيء ونفس تأمره بآخر (maqayis)؛ آمرته في أمري إذا شاورته، والائتمار والاستئمار المشاورة وكذلك التآمر (sihah)؛ ائتمر القوم إذا تشاوروا، أي كيف يرتئي رأيا ويشاور نفسه ويعقد عليه (tahdhib)؛ الائتمار قبول الأمر، ويقال للتشاور ائتمار (mufradat)
- **B008** zayıf görüşlü kişi — görüşü zayıf, her sözü dinleyip uyan akılsız kişi
  الإمر الرجل الضعيف الرأي الأحمق الذي يسمع كلام هذا وكلام هذا (maqayis)؛ الإمر الضعيف من الرجال (ayn)؛ رجل إمر وإمرة أي ضعيف الرأي يأتمر لكل أحد (sihah)؛ رجل إمر وإمرة أي يستأمر كل أحد في أمره، والإمر الأحمق (tahdhib)
- **B009** küçük koyun yavrusu — küçük koyun yavrusu; dişisi dişi kuzu veya genç dişi koyun
  الإمرة الأنثى من الحملان (ayn)؛ الإمر الصغير من ولد الضأن والأنثى إمرة (sihah)؛ الإمر الخروف والإمرة الرخل (tahdhib)
- **B010** Tanrı'ya özgü yaratma — Tanrı'ya özgü yaratma ve var etme
  ويقال للإبداع أمر، ويختص ذلك بالله تعالى دون الخلائق؛ قل الروح من أمر ربي أي من إبداعه؛ إنما قولنا لشيء إذا أردناه أن نقول له كن فيكون
- **B011** mızrağa uç takma — sivriltilmiş veya uç takılmış mızrak ucu · mızrağına keskin uç tak
  سنان مؤمر أي محدد؛ أمر قناتك أي اجعل فيها سنانا

## ء ل ه (root_000047): 82:19 لِّلَّهِ

- **B001** tapınma ve tapınılan varlık — tapınmak · kendini tapınmaya vermek · tapınılır kılmak · tapınılan varlık, tanrı · tapınılan varlık · tanrılar, tapınılan nesneler · tapınma · kimi toplulukların tapındığı için bu adla anılan güneş · senin tapınman
  أصل واحد وهو التعبد، فالإله الله تعالى لأنه معبود، وتأله الرجل إذا تعبد، والإلاهة الشمس سميت بذلك لأن قوما كانوا يعبدونها (maqayis)؛ التأله التعبد (ayn)؛ أله بالفتح إلاهة أي عبد عبادة، مألوه أي معبود، الآلهة الأصنام، التأليه التعبيد، التأله التنسك والتعبد (sihah)؛ لا يكون إلاها حتى يكون معبودا، معبوداتهم من الأصنام والأوثان آلهة، الإلاهة الشمس، وإلاهتك وعبادتك (tahdhib)؛ إله اسما لكل معبود، أله فلان يأله الآلهة عبد، فالإله على هذا هو المعبود (mufradat)
- **B002** Yaratıcıya özgü ad ile seslenme ve ant biçimleri — Yaratıcıya özgü ad · Tanrı adına ant olsun, bunu yapmadım · ey Tanrı; yakarma seslenişi · ey Tanrı; doğrudan seslenme · Tanrı adına sen veya baban; şaşma ya da ant kalıbı · Tanrı adına, gerçekten sen veya biz; kısaltılmış ant kalıpları
  فالإله الله تعالى وسمي بذلك لأنه معبود (maqayis)؛ اسم الله الأكبر هو الله، الله ما فعلت ذاك تريد والله ما فعلته، لاه أنت أي لله أنت، لا هم اغفر لنا (ayn)؛ منه قولنا الله وأصله إلاه، يا ألله اغفر لي (sihah)؛ اسم الله الأكبر هو الله، الله ما فعلت تريد والله، اللهم بمعنى يا ألله، لاه أبوك، لهنك، لهنا، يا ألله اغفر لي (tahdhib)؛ الله قيل أصله إله فحذفت همزته وأدخل عليها الألف واللام فخص بالباري تعالى (mufradat)

## و ل ه (root_005296): documented alternative for 82:19 لِّلَّهِ: Ebû’l-Heysem; Ezherî’nin Tehzîbü’l-luğa’daki aktarımı

- **B001** yoğun duygudan aklı karışma veya ayrılıktan özlem çekme — 
  أصل صحيح يدل على اضطراب شيء أو ذهابه (maqayis)؛ رجل واله وامرأة واله ووالهة (maqayis;sihah)؛ الوله ذهاب العقل والتحير من شدة الوجد (sihah)؛ ولهت إليه تله أن تحن إليه (tahdhib)؛ الوله يكون من الحزن والسرور مثل الطرب (tahdhib)؛ ناقة واله إذا اشتد وجدها على ولدها (sihah)؛ الجمل إذا فقد ألافه فحن إليها واله (tahdhib)؛ البلاد التي توله الإنسان أي تحيره (sihah)
- **B002** anneyi yavrusundan ayırıp özleme düşürme — 
  التوليه أن يفرق بين المرأة وولدها (maqayis;sihah)؛ لا توله والدة عن ولدها (maqayis;tahdhib)؛ التوليه أن يفرق بينهما في البيع وكل أنثى فارقت ولدها فهي واله (tahdhib)؛ لا تجعل والها وذلك في السبايا (sihah)
- **B003** salınan suyun açık arazide akıp kaybolması — 
  عين مولهة إذا أرسل ماؤها فذهب في الصحارى (maqayis)؛ ماء موله وموله أرسل في الصحراء فذهب (sihah)



===== _commentary/v16/work/s082/surah.r2/channels.md =====
(source: latent_activation/network/v3/reviews/s082/reader_a_pilot.md)

# S082 Semantic Channel Discovery

## Parent Channels

### 1. Sky, Height, and Atmospheric Display
- Semantic invariant: Things become perceptible through elevation, overhead layering, celestial light, or weather-borne display.
- Surface relation: direct; the sky and stars anchor 82:1-2, while the same roots recur through latent senses at 82:6-8 and 82:13.
- Surprising reach: The sky scene extends into layered cloud, nocturnal moisture, calendrical visibility, public reputation, and animals used as celestial analogues.

#### Subchannel A. Overhead Canopy and Layered Cloud
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An elevated canopy covers the scene while cloud masses gather and mount in layers beneath it.
- Active motifs: elevated height `س م و:B001/m01`; high visible figure `س م و:B002/m01`; sky or overhead canopy `س م و:B004/m01`; layered rain cloud `ر ب ب:B008/m01`; one mass mounted upon another `ر ك ب:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: س م و at 82:1; ر ب ب at 82:6; ر ك ب at 82:8.
- Synthesis: Height supplies the spatial frame, the canopy supplies enclosure, and cloud plus superposition supplies atmospheric structure. The surface sky therefore opens into a coherent meteorological scene rather than a bare location.

#### Subchannel B. Stars, Lunar Stations, and Calendar Beginnings
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Celestial lights mark visible positions and recurrent divisions of the month.
- Active motifs: star in the sky `ك و ك ب:B001/m01`; white celestial patch `ك و ك ب:B003/m01`; lunar mansion called the Lion's nose `ن ث ر:B004/m01`; night of the balanced full moon `س و ي:B012/m01`; first appearance or beginning of a month `غ ر ر:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك و ك ب and ن ث ر at 82:2; س و ي at 82:7; غ ر ر at 82:6.
- Synthesis: The stars provide lights, the lunar mansion provides a fixed celestial station, and the balanced moon plus first-of-month sense provides calendrical sequence. Their scattering at 82:2 resonates with an ordered sky whose markers can also be displaced.

#### Subchannel C. Night Moisture, Southern Breeze, and Fertile Rain
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Moisture arrives at night or on a mild southern wind and supports fertile land.
- Active motifs: nighttime droplets on grass `ك و ك ب:B004/m01`; mild moist south wind `ن ع م:B009/m01`; rain-bearing generous cloud `ك ر م:B002/m01`; sky as rain source `س م و:B004/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ك و ك ب at 82:2; ن ع م at 82:13; ك ر م at 82:6/11; س م و at 82:1.
- Synthesis: Dew, soft wind, and rain form one atmospheric delivery process. The channel connects the elevated sky to the moisture by which terrestrial fertility becomes visible.

#### Subchannel D. Bright Prominence and Public Renown
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person or place stands out as a bright, elevated, or widely noticed presence.
- Active motifs: good renown carried abroad `س م و:B008/m01`; brightness of a meadow `ك و ك ب:B006/m01`; conspicuous whiteness `غ ر ر:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: س م و at 82:1; ك و ك ب at 82:2; غ ر ر at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Physical visibility and social visibility share the invariant of standing out from a field. A bright patch, luminous meadow, and good reputation all become forms of perceptible prominence.

#### Subchannel E. Terrestrial Figures Mirroring the Sky
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Animal height, whiteness, or shape is described through comparison with celestial forms.
- Active motifs: bull likened to a star `ك و ك ب:B002/m01`; ostrich-like and lunar-station forms `ن ع م:B007/m01`; stallion rising over its herd `س م و:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك و ك ب at 82:2; ن ع م at 82:13; س م و at 82:1.
- Synthesis: The scene is analogical rather than astronomical: terrestrial bodies reproduce celestial height, silhouette, or brilliance. It preserves a recurring shape bridge between sky imagery and animal form.

### 2. Rupture, Dispersal, and Exposure
- Semantic invariant: A closed, ordered, or concealed arrangement is opened, scattered, overturned, or made visible.
- Surface relation: direct; 82:1-4 presents sky rupture, stellar dispersal, water release, and grave excavation.
- Surprising reach: The same mechanics reach folded material, worn cloth, confused crowds, migrating groups, and overturned basins.

#### Subchannel A. Fissure and Forceful Opening
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A surface splits and creates a broad opening through which what was contained can emerge.
- Active motifs: opening or splitting `ف ط ر:B001/m01`; broad fissure and issuing flow `ف ج ر:B001/m01`; slit or wide cut `ب ح ر:B003/m01`; visible cleft in an upper lip `ع ل م:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ف ط ر at 82:1; ف ج ر and ب ح ر at 82:3; ع ل م at 82:5/12.
- Synthesis: Each motif supplies a distinct material instance of the same operation: an intact boundary becomes a passage. The bodily cleft and cut ear preserve the concrete morphology of the cosmic and hydrological rupture.

#### Subchannel B. Scattering, Tumult, and Group Dispersal
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Ordered contents are thrown apart, producing noise, confusion, and separation.
- Active motifs: strewing separate pieces `ن ث ر:B001/m01`; goods overturned and dispersed `ب ع ث ر:B002/m01`; agitation and mixed voices `ب ل ل:B009/m01`; people dispersing under every star `ك و ك ب:B008/m01`; a group departing in rout `ن ع م:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ث ر and ك و ك ب at 82:2; ب ع ث ر at 82:4; ب ل ل at 82:9; ن ع م at 82:13.
- Synthesis: Strewing is the core operation, while noise and routed movement describe its social aftermath. The stellar surface scene thus resonates with disturbed possessions, speech, and communities losing cohesion.

#### Subchannel C. Exhumation and Revealed Burial
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Earth covering a body is turned back so the buried occupant becomes exposed.
- Active motifs: grave and burial `ق ب ر:B001/m01`; turning soil over a buried thing `ب ع ث ر:B001/m01`; placing or uncovering a body in burial `غ ي ب:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ق ب ر and ب ع ث ر at 82:4; غ ي ب at 82:16.
- Synthesis: The grave supplies the setting, burial supplies concealment, and turned soil supplies reversal. Together they form the explicit mortuary scene of 82:4 and its latent counter-movement from disappearance to exposure.

#### Subchannel D. Overturned Hollow and Collapsed Basin
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A containing hollow loses its orientation or enclosure when its lower part is brought upward.
- Active motifs: basin demolished and inverted `ب ع ث ر:B003/m01`; enclosed or sunken cavity `ق ب ر:B002/m01`; low hollow where an object disappears `غ ي ب:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ع ث ر and ق ب ر at 82:4; غ ي ب at 82:16.
- Synthesis: Hollow, containment, and inversion form one topological mechanism. It extends grave-opening imagery to any depression or basin whose concealed interior is exposed by overturning.

#### Subchannel E. Crease, Wear, and Concealed Material Damage
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Folding leaves a trace, and repeated use turns that trace into wear or a hidden defect.
- Active motifs: visible crease left by a fold `غ ر ر:B001/m01`; folding while damp and carrying a flaw `ب ل ل:B007/m01`; cloth worn smooth, frayed, or threadbare `خ ل ق:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ر ر at 82:6; ب ل ل at 82:9; خ ل ق at 82:7.
- Synthesis: The crease is the first material disturbance, damp folding preserves it, and wear is its durable outcome. The channel is a small-scale material analogue of larger rupture and exposure.

### 3. Water, Moisture, and Sustenance
- Semantic invariant: Water gathers, spreads, moistens, fills, or sustains life while its absence produces thirst.
- Surface relation: direct; the seas and their release anchor 82:3, with latent water senses distributed across 82:5-7, 82:13, and 82:19.
- Surprising reach: Water appears not only as sea and pool but as a travel resource, a measured draught, a filled vessel, and a determinant of settlement.

#### Subchannel A. Broad and Saline Waters
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A large body of water spreads across an open expanse and may become strongly saline.
- Active motifs: abundant broad water `ب ح ر:B001/m01`; salinity or brine `ب ح ر:B004/m01`; deep gathered water `ع ل م:B005/m01`; abundant collected water `ر ب ب:B013/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ح ر at 82:3; ع ل م at 82:5/12; ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Breadth, depth, gathering, and salinity describe the physical state of a sea or deep well. They amplify the surface plural seas into a full hydrological body.

#### Subchannel B. Pools, Wells, and Water-Holding Ground
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Depressed ground or rock receives and retains water for later use.
- Active motifs: low ground that gathers water `ب ح ر:B006/m01`; rock hollow or new well retaining rain `خ ل ق:B011/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ح ر at 82:3; خ ل ق at 82:7.
- Synthesis: Depression supplies the site and the rock hollow supplies the container. This is the contained counterpart to the released seas of 82:3.

#### Subchannel C. Water as the Mainstay of Travel and Life
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A traveler or community secures enough water to maintain movement and continued life.
- Active motifs: water by which a party controls its affairs `م ل ك:B007/m01`; water that sustains the self `ن ف س:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: م ل ك and ن ف س at 82:19.
- Synthesis: Water is both substance and operational mainstay. Possessing it changes a journey from exposure to viable movement, making hydrology a form of practical sovereignty.

#### Subchannel D. Filling, Wetting, and Pouring
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Liquid is driven into a vessel or surface until it becomes full or wet.
- Active motifs: filling a waterskin or feeding liquid inward `غ ر ر:B011/m01`; wetness and remaining moisture `ب ل ل:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ر ر at 82:6; ب ل ل at 82:9.
- Synthesis: The vessel supplies containment, filling supplies movement, and wetness supplies the resulting state. The process bridges water flow with storage.

#### Subchannel E. Thirst and the Measured Draught
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A thirsty body takes water in discrete draughts yet may remain unsatisfied.
- Active motifs: severe thirst without satiation `ب ح ر:B014/m01`; a breath-sized draught or pause in drinking `ن ف س:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ح ر at 82:3; ن ف س at 82:5/19.
- Synthesis: The draught gives the unit of consumption and unrelieved thirst gives the failed outcome. The pair preserves water as a bodily need rather than only a landscape.

### 4. Creation, Form, and Development
- Semantic invariant: An entity is originated, measured, assembled, proportioned, and brought toward a mature form.
- Surface relation: direct; creation, proportion, adjustment, image, will, and composition appear at 82:7-8.
- Surprising reach: The formation sequence extends into planning, fitted components, premature material, emerging teeth, smooth surfaces, and blocked forms.

#### Subchannel A. Origination and Effective Making
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A maker brings an entity or action into occurrence from an initial act of origination.
- Active motifs: first origination `ف ط ر:B002/m01`; creating or bringing into existence `خ ل ق:B002/m01`; effective production of an act `ف ع ل:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ف ط ر at 82:1; خ ل ق at 82:7; ف ع ل at 82:12.
- Synthesis: Origination supplies the beginning, creation supplies the maker-object relation, and effective action supplies realization. These motifs form the causal core behind the formed human of 82:7-8.

#### Subchannel B. Measure, Design, and Assembly
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Parts are measured before action, joined, and fixed in their proper positions.
- Active motifs: prior measuring and estimation `خ ل ق:B001/m01`; joining one thing to another `ك ت ب:B001/m01`; fitted component in a composite `ر ك ب:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: خ ل ق at 82:7; ك ت ب at 82:11; ر ك ب at 82:8.
- Synthesis: Measurement defines intended dimensions, joining produces structure, and fitting fixes each component. The sequence gives concrete mechanics to the surface statement that the human was composed.

#### Subchannel C. Proportion, Balance, and Visible Form
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A created body is straightened, balanced, and made perceptible in a definite image.
- Active motifs: completed and visibly proportioned form `خ ل ق:B003/m01`; straight and complete constitution `س و ي:B002/m01`; bodily rectification `ع د ل:B005/m01`; perceptible created image `ص و ر:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: خ ل ق, س و ي, and ع د ل at 82:7; ص و ر at 82:8.
- Synthesis: Completion, straightness, balance, and image are successive descriptions of one formed body. This is the most direct lexical expansion of 82:7-8.

#### Subchannel D. Willed Variation, Distortion, and Surface Quality
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A form may be selected, made attractive or distorted, and given a smooth or irregular surface.
- Active motifs: creative volition `ش ي ء:B001/m01`; distorted or ugly configuration `ش ي ء:B002/m01`; smooth even surface `خ ل ق:B008/m01`; imperforate or blocked form `خ ل ق:B012/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ش ي ء at 82:8/19; خ ل ق at 82:7.
- Synthesis: Volition selects the configuration, while distortion, smoothness, and blockage mark materially different outcomes of formation. Their contrast preserves variation without reducing every form to an idealized body.

#### Subchannel E. Nurture, Maturation, and Premature Emergence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A living or worked thing develops by stages but can emerge before its process is complete.
- Active motifs: gradual nurture and completion `ر ب ب:B002/m01`; attainment of mature strength `س و ي:B005/m01`; dough or material used before ripening `ف ط ر:B006/m01`; tooth breaking through `ف ط ر:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ب ب at 82:6; س و ي and خ ل ق at 82:7; ف ط ر at 82:1.
- Synthesis: Nurture describes sustained development, maturity its expected end, and premature use or tooth eruption the moment of incomplete or forceful emergence. The scene extends creation into temporal development.

### 5. Knowledge, Perception, Signs, and Records
- Semantic invariant: Information becomes available through perception, learning, memory, marking, or durable inscription.
- Surface relation: direct; knowing appears at 82:5 and 82:12, guardians and writers at 82:10-11, and the limits of knowing at 82:17-18.
- Surprising reach: Knowledge is embodied in hearing, memory, road signs, templates, banners, registers, and the tension between hiddenness and doubt.

#### Subchannel A. Knowing, Learning, and Sage Formation
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A knower apprehends information, develops expertise, and can transmit it.
- Active motifs: disclosed knowledge `ع ل م:B001/m01`; practical knowing and informing `د ر ي:B001/m01`; cultivated learned person `ر ب ب:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ع ل م at 82:5/12; د ر ي at 82:17/18; ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Apprehension supplies the cognitive event, practical knowledge supplies mastery, and cultivated learning supplies the formed knower. Together they expand the repeated epistemic challenge of the surah.

#### Subchannel B. Detection, Hearing, and Farsight
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A perceiver detects an object through sight, sound, sensation, or long-range observation.
- Active motifs: seeing, hearing, or sensing a presence `ء ن س:B002/m01`; deliberate listening `ش ي ء:B005/m01`; long sight `ش ي ء:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ء ن س at 82:6; ش ي ء at 82:8/19.
- Synthesis: Detection names the general perceptual act, listening its auditory mode, and farsight its extended visual mode. The channel preserves perception as a route into knowledge rather than conflating it with learned cognition.

#### Subchannel C. Memory, Vigilance, and Inner Comprehension
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Information is retained inwardly while the subject remains alert against loss or error.
- Active motifs: retained content and faculty of memory `ح ف ظ:B002/m01`; vigilant self-guarding `ح ف ظ:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ح ف ظ at 82:10.
- Synthesis: Memory preserves content and vigilance protects its use. This makes guarding at 82:10 resonate with cognitive custody as well as external surveillance.

#### Subchannel D. Marks, Banners, Landmarks, and Templates
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A visible sign distinguishes an object, guides a traveler, or provides a model for repeated work.
- Active motifs: distinguishing mark, banner, or landmark `ع ل م:B002/m01`; road sign or appointed marker `ء م ر:B005/m01`; model and single guiding course `غ ر ر:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ع ل م at 82:5/12; ء م ر at 82:19; غ ر ر at 82:6.
- Synthesis: A mark identifies, a landmark or appointment orients, and a template governs repetition. The scene unifies semiotic and practical guidance through a visible differentiator.

#### Subchannel E. Guardianship, Writing, and Enrollment
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Observed acts are guarded, written, and entered into a durable register.
- Active motifs: entrusted guardianship `ح ف ظ:B001/m01`; written text or document `ك ت ب:B002/m01`; entering a name in a register `ك ت ب:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ح ف ظ at 82:10; ك ت ب at 82:11.
- Synthesis: Guardians supply continuous observation, writing fixes the information, and enrollment assigns it to a named subject or class. This gives institutional form to the record scene of 82:10-12.

#### Subchannel F. Unseen Content and Epistemic Doubt
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Information is unavailable because it is concealed, absent, or held in doubt.
- Active motifs: hidden from sight or knowledge `غ ي ب:B001/m01`; unseen construed as doubt `غ ي ب:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ي ب at 82:16.
- Synthesis: Hiddenness describes inaccessible evidence, while doubt describes the knower's resulting state. Their relation clarifies why absence can become an epistemic problem without making absence and uncertainty identical.

### 6. Judgment, Balance, and Deferred Exchange
- Semantic invariant: Claims, persons, or obligations are assessed through judgment, equivalence, counterweight, deliberation, or settlement.
- Surface relation: direct; adjustment appears at 82:7, judgment and recompense at 82:9 and 82:15-19.
- Surprising reach: Judicial balance extends into ransom, balanced loads, credit risk, debt records, and delegated credibility.

#### Subchannel A. Judgment and Binding Determination
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: An authority evaluates conduct and fixes a binding outcome.
- Active motifs: reckoning, judgment, and recompense `د ي ن:B002/m01`; just adjudication `ع د ل:B001/m01`; binding written decree `ك ت ب:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: د ي ن at 82:9/15/17/18; ع د ل at 82:7; ك ت ب at 82:11.
- Synthesis: Reckoning supplies the case, justice supplies the norm, and the written decree fixes the result. The channel captures the institutional mechanics implied by the repeated Day of Judgment.

#### Subchannel B. Equality, Parity, and a Fair Center
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Two parties or quantities are brought into equivalent relation around an impartial middle.
- Active motifs: equivalence between two things `ع د ل:B002/m01`; parity and equal valuation `س و ي:B001/m01`; fair and known middle `س و ي:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ع د ل and س و ي at 82:7.
- Synthesis: Equivalence compares the parties, parity states their relation, and the fair middle supplies a neutral position. The surface bodily adjustment therefore resonates with mathematical and judicial balance.

#### Subchannel C. Ransom and Equivalent Substitute
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: One thing is accepted in place of another because it matches its value.
- Active motifs: ransom or equivalent substitute `ع د ل:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ع د ل at 82:7.
- Synthesis: Substitution is a legal-economic relation in which a second item can stand against the first because it supplies an accepted equivalent.

#### Subchannel D. Deliberation Between Alternatives
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A decision-maker considers competing courses before choosing one.
- Active motifs: weighing two alternatives `ع د ل:B008/m01`; consultation and managed opinion `ء م ر:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ع د ل at 82:7; ء م ر at 82:19.
- Synthesis: Deliberation supplies comparison and consultation supplies social reasoning. The scene is the pre-judgment process by which an outcome is selected rather than imposed blindly.

#### Subchannel E. Debt, Credit, and Deferred Settlement
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A transfer occurs now while payment, release, or completion remains due later.
- Active motifs: financial debt and credit `د ي ن:B003/m01`; sale or payment deferred to a later time `ء خ ر:B002/m01`; uncertain commercial outcome `غ ر ر:B009/m01`; emancipation contract paid by installments `ك ت ب:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: د ي ن at 82:9/15/17/18; ء خ ر at 82:5; غ ر ر at 82:6; ك ت ب at 82:11.
- Synthesis: Debt establishes obligation, deferment separates exchange from settlement, risk clouds the outcome, and the installment contract turns payment into release. The scene is coherent commercial temporality rather than generic lateness.

#### Subchannel F. Delegated Credibility
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person's word or oath is accepted and a determination is entrusted to that credibility.
- Active motifs: authorizing a person's judgment or oath `د ي ن:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: د ي ن at 82:9/15/17/18.
- Synthesis: Delegation transfers decision authority by crediting a person's judgment, oath, or religious responsibility.

### 7. Sovereignty, Possession, and Polity
- Semantic invariant: Affairs are ordered through command, ownership, rule, institutional settlement, and recognized leadership.
- Surface relation: direct; lordship appears at 82:6 and exclusive command and non-possession at 82:19.
- Surprising reach: Sovereignty reaches city organization, practical mainstays, animal leaders, maritime command, and the vulnerable follower who depends on others' decisions.

#### Subchannel A. Lordship, Kingship, and Command
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A sovereign possesses recognized authority over subjects and affairs.
- Active motifs: kingship and political sovereignty `م ل ك:B003/m01`; lordship, mastery, and ownership `ر ب ب:B001/m01`; office of rule `ء م ر:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: م ل ك and ء م ر at 82:19; ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Lordship supplies the master-subject relation, kingship supplies public sovereignty, and office supplies institutional form. The convergence sharpens the transfer of all command to God in 82:19.

#### Subchannel B. Property, Disposal, and Subjugation
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A possessor controls an object or person and can direct its use.
- Active motifs: ownership and power of disposal `م ل ك:B002/m01`; coercive possession and subjugation `د ي ن:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: م ل ك at 82:19; د ي ن at 82:9/15/17/18.
- Synthesis: Property describes recognized control, while subjugation reveals its coercive extreme. Their contrast matters at 82:19, where no self controls anything for another.

#### Subchannel C. City, Administration, and Institutional Mainstay
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A settled polity maintains obedience through an organized center and a sustaining principle.
- Active motifs: city where authority is obeyed `د ي ن:B006/m01`; mainstay on which an affair depends `م ل ك:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: د ي ن at 82:9/15/17/18; م ل ك at 82:19.
- Synthesis: The city provides territory, obedience provides political relation, and the mainstay provides structural continuity. This is rule embodied in a functioning settlement.

#### Subchannel D. Vanguard, Animal Guide, and Shipmaster
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A leading figure moves first and directs a following body.
- Active motifs: animal leader followed by the herd `م ل ك:B008/m01`; forepart and vanguard `ق د م:B007/m01`; chief of sailors `ر ب ب:B017/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: م ل ك at 82:19; ق د م at 82:5; ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Vanguard gives spatial precedence, the herd leader gives follower structure, and the shipmaster gives skilled command. They are concrete analogues of authority as directional leadership.

#### Subchannel E. Command, Compliance, and Dependent Following
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: An order is issued and a subordinate either complies from judgment or follows because judgment is weak.
- Active motifs: binding command and request `ء م ر:B002/m01`; weak-minded follower seeking everyone's direction `ء م ر:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ء م ر at 82:19.
- Synthesis: The same root supplies both authoritative order and deficient dependence. The contrast distinguishes principled obedience from indiscriminate compliance.

### 8. Worship, Observance, and Covenant
- Semantic invariant: A subject binds conduct to a sacred authority through worship, prayer, repeated observance, or protected covenant.
- Surface relation: direct; lordship at 82:6, denial of religion at 82:9, guarded and written acts at 82:10-12, and final divine command at 82:19.
- Surprising reach: The sacred frame includes houses of worship, breaking a fast, protective honor, treaty, and sacrilege.

#### Subchannel A. Worship and Religious Obedience
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A worshipper submits to a deity through an established religious practice.
- Active motifs: deity and act of worship `ء ل ه:B001/m01`; religious obedience and creed `د ي ن:B001/m01`; prescribed prayer `ص ل ي:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ء ل ه at 82:19; د ي ن at 82:9/15/17/18; ص ل ي at 82:15.
- Synthesis: The deity supplies the object, religion the binding order, and prayer the repeated act. These roles form a complete devotional scene.

#### Subchannel B. Supplication, Blessing, and Sacred Invocation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A speaker invokes God or asks blessing and mercy for another.
- Active motifs: prayer, blessing, and mercy `ص ل ي:B002/m01`; divine name in invocation `ء ل ه:B002/m01`; gracious prayer-response formula `ك ر م:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ص ل ي at 82:15; ء ل ه at 82:19; ك ر م at 82:6/11.
- Synthesis: Invocation names the addressed authority, supplication performs the request, and the gracious response completes the exchange. The channel joins sacred speech with beneficent social address.

#### Subchannel C. Repeated Observance and Ritual Place
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Worship is maintained through regular observance in a designated place, with fasting marked by its permitted release.
- Active motifs: persistent observance `ح ف ظ:B003/m01`; worship place or shrine `ص ل ي:B008/m01`; ending or breaking a fast `ف ط ر:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ح ف ظ at 82:10; ص ل ي at 82:15; ف ط ر at 82:1.
- Synthesis: Persistence supplies temporal discipline, the shrine supplies setting, and breaking the fast marks a ritual boundary. These are practical structures of sustained religious life.

#### Subchannel D. Covenant, Protected Honor, and Sacrilege
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A binding pact protects persons and sacred limits until violation turns the relation into sacrilege.
- Active motifs: treaty and protected compact `ر ب ب:B011/m01`; safeguarding covenant and inviolable honor `ح ف ظ:B006/m01`; violation of sacred limits in the Days of Fijar `ف ج ر:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ب ب at 82:6; ح ف ظ at 82:10; ف ج ر at 82:3/14.
- Synthesis: Covenant establishes obligation, protection maintains it, and sacrilege names its rupture. The scene preserves fidelity and violation as opposite outcomes of one sacred social frame.

### 9. Virtue, Benefaction, and Honor
- Semantic invariant: Good character becomes socially effective through truth, generosity, aid, praise, dignity, and gracious response.
- Surface relation: direct; the generous Lord and honorable writers appear at 82:6 and 82:11, while the righteous and blissful appear at 82:13.
- Surprising reach: Moral excellence extends into abundant flow, social welfare, prior merit, reputation, gift exchange, and verbal courtesy.

#### Subchannel A. Truthful and Righteous Action
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A person fulfills speech and action in accordance with goodness and obedience.
- Active motifs: truth carried through in word and deed `ب ر ر:B001/m01`; broad goodness and righteousness `ب ر ر:B002/m01`; beneficent or blameworthy quality of action `ف ع ل:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ر ر at 82:13; ف ع ل at 82:12.
- Synthesis: Truth gives fidelity, righteousness gives moral orientation, and action makes character observable. The scene grounds the righteous of 82:13 in enacted conduct.

#### Subchannel B. Generosity as Abundant Increase
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Benefit flows beyond sufficiency and enlarges the recipient's condition.
- Active motifs: generosity pouring forth widely `ف ج ر:B005/m01`; noble generosity `ك ر م:B001/m01`; increase beyond the initial act `ن ع م:B010/m01`; growth and blessing `ء م ر:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ف ج ر at 82:3/14; ك ر م at 82:6/11; ن ع م at 82:13; ء م ر at 82:19.
- Synthesis: Flow supplies the image, nobility the agent quality, increase the operation, and blessing the outcome. The cluster makes generosity a productive process rather than a static trait.

#### Subchannel C. Welfare, Aid, and Favor
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A need is met by benefit, support, or a favor that restores good condition.
- Active motifs: material benefit and social support `ب ل ل:B002/m01`; need, favor, or tightly held benefit `ر ب ب:B016/m01`; good condition and bestowed ease `ن ع م:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ل ل at 82:9; ر ب ب at 82:6; ن ع م at 82:13.
- Synthesis: Need supplies the deficit, support supplies intervention, and ease supplies the resulting condition. This is benefaction viewed from the recipient's welfare.

#### Subchannel D. Honor, Prior Merit, and Elevated Standing
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person receives standing because of noble quality or an established record of merit.
- Active motifs: honor and noble excellence `ك ر م:B001/m02`; prior merit or distinguished precedence `ق د م:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك ر م at 82:6/11; ق د م at 82:5.
- Synthesis: Noble quality supplies intrinsic worth and prior merit supplies an established history. Together they distinguish earned standing from mere display.

#### Subchannel E. Gift, Praise, and Courteous Response
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A gift or commendation invites acknowledgment, reward, or a gracious verbal reply.
- Active motifs: gift offered in expectation of recompense `ك ر م:B008/m01`; courteous answer and willing acceptance `ك ر م:B009/m02`; explicit praise or commendation `ن ع م:B003/m01`; prayer for comfort of the eye `ن ع م:B013/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك ر م at 82:6/11; ن ع م at 82:13.
- Synthesis: Gift initiates exchange, praise evaluates it, courtesy answers it, and blessing extends goodwill to the recipient. The scene joins material and verbal forms of honor.

### 10. Falsehood, Deception, and Breach
- Semantic invariant: Appearance, speech, or conduct presents something other than the truth or fails a trusted relation.
- Surface relation: direct; delusion appears at 82:6, denial at 82:9, and the wicked at 82:14.
- Surprising reach: Deception includes fabricated language, misleading cloth and shine, naivety, hazardous exchange, concealed flaws, and a soul characterized by falsehood.

#### Subchannel A. Fabrication and Imputed Falsehood
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A statement or artifact is invented and then asserted or attributed as true.
- Active motifs: false statement or act `ك ذ ب:B001/m01`; imputing falsehood to a speaker `ك ذ ب:B002/m01`; invented speech or story `خ ل ق:B007/m01`; fabricated verse, report, or song `ف ع ل:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك ذ ب at 82:9; خ ل ق at 82:7; ف ع ل at 82:12.
- Synthesis: Invention produces the false content, assertion presents it, and imputation governs who is called false. The channel covers both making a lie and socially assigning it.

#### Subchannel B. Misleading Appearance
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An attractive or patterned surface claims a condition it does not actually possess.
- Active motifs: lure through transient glitter or ornament `غ ر ر:B008/m01`; patterned cloth that lies by its appearance `ك ذ ب:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ر ر at 82:6; ك ذ ب at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Glitter supplies attraction and the patterned cloth supplies a concrete deceptive object. Both make visual appeal the vehicle of false representation.

#### Subchannel C. Naivety, Bewilderment, and Hazard
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An inexperienced or confused actor enters a situation whose outcome is uncertain.
- Active motifs: inexperience and easy credulity `غ ر ر:B007/m01`; hazard with unknown outcome `غ ر ر:B009/m02`; bewilderment or stupefaction `ب ح ر:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ر ر at 82:6; ب ح ر at 82:3.
- Synthesis: Inexperience weakens judgment, bewilderment disturbs it, and hazard defines the uncertain environment. This is the cognitive mechanism by which a lure can succeed.

#### Subchannel D. Concealed Fault and Moral Breach
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A person hides a defect or violates a protected standard while preserving an outward appearance of continuity.
- Active motifs: moral violation and torn restraint `ف ج ر:B004/m01`; folding over a retained defect `ب ل ل:B007/m02`; failure to preserve protected honor `ح ف ظ:B006/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ف ج ر at 82:3/14; ب ل ل at 82:9; ح ف ظ at 82:10.
- Synthesis: The concealed fault supplies covert defect, violated restraint supplies the act, and lost protection supplies the social consequence. The scene joins material concealment to ethical breach.

#### Subchannel E. The False or Self-Deceiving Soul
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Falsehood is attributed not only to a statement but to the inward self that generates or accepts it.
- Active motifs: soul characterized as false `ك ذ ب:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك ذ ب at 82:9.
- Synthesis: The false soul internalizes deception as a character state rather than confining it to a single utterance.

### 11. Time, Sequence, and Decisive Event
- Semantic invariant: Things are ordered by duration, beginning, precedence, delay, recurrence, or a singular event that changes the state of affairs.
- Surface relation: direct; before and after appear at 82:5, the Day of Judgment at 82:15 and 82:17-19.
- Surprising reach: Temporal order includes dawn, ancient duration, delayed transactions, habitual practice, sudden multitudes, medical crisis, and arrival from travel.

#### Subchannel A. Day, Duration, and Antiquity
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A bounded day sits within a longer span that may extend into remote antiquity.
- Active motifs: daylight period `ي و م:B001/m01`; unspecified duration or age `ي و م:B002/m01`; ancient time opposed to new occurrence `ق د م:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ي و م at 82:15/17/18/19; ق د م at 82:5.
- Synthesis: The day gives a bounded unit, duration broadens it, and antiquity extends it backward. Together they distinguish event time from long historical span.

#### Subchannel B. Dawn and Expanding Beginning
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Light opens from darkness and the day begins to expand.
- Active motifs: dawn breaking from night `ف ج ر:B002/m01`; morning or day breathing open `ن ف س:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ف ج ر at 82:3/14; ن ف س at 82:5/19.
- Synthesis: Dawn supplies visible emergence, while breathing supplies the shape of gradual expansion. The pair forms a coherent beginning process rather than a static timestamp.

#### Subchannel C. Before, After, and Deferred Completion
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: Acts occupy ordered positions, with some advanced and others left until later.
- Active motifs: moving or placing something ahead `ق د م:B004/m01`; temporal laterness `ء خ ر:B001/m01`; postponement to a later time `ء خ ر:B002/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ق د م and ء خ ر at 82:5.
- Synthesis: Precedence fixes the earlier position, laterness supplies contrast, and postponement makes the relation intentional. This is the direct temporal architecture of 82:5.

#### Subchannel D. Crisis, Calamity, and the Decisive Day
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A severe occurrence arrives, gathers force, and defines a particular day by its outcome.
- Active motifs: day as great event or ordeal `ي و م:B003/m01`; grave and astonishing affair `ء م ر:B006/m01`; sudden arrival of a multitude or calamity `ف ج ر:B003/m01`; acute crisis day `ب ح ر:B012/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ي و م at 82:15/17/18/19; ء م ر at 82:19; ف ج ر at 82:3/14; ب ح ر at 82:3.
- Synthesis: The event supplies severity, sudden arrival supplies transition, and the crisis day supplies a bounded decisive interval. The repeated Day of Judgment resonates with other days named by what overtakes those within them.

#### Subchannel E. Habit, Continuity, and Residence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A condition persists because an actor repeats it or remains in place.
- Active motifs: customary state or habitual practice `د ي ن:B005/m01`; staying and enduring in a place `ر ب ب:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: د ي ن at 82:9/15/17/18; ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Habit provides repeated action and residence provides sustained presence. Their shared invariant is continuity across time.

#### Subchannel F. Prompt Action and Arrival
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An actor acts without delay or reaches the destination after travel.
- Active motifs: not lingering before an act `ك ذ ب:B005/m01`; arrival or return from travel `ق د م:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك ذ ب at 82:9; ق د م at 82:5.
- Synthesis: Promptness measures the interval before action, while arrival closes the interval of travel. Both define completion by the absence of further delay.

### 12. Orientation, Travel, and Pursuit
- Semantic invariant: A participant adopts a direction, follows a route, moves by foot or mount, or pursues a target.
- Surface relation: indirect; advancement at 82:5, composition and mounting imagery at 82:8, and control of movement at 82:19 provide anchors.
- Surprising reach: Orientation extends into body-sidedness, road centers, sea travel, hunting concealment, accidental encounter, capture, and balanced riding gear.

#### Subchannel A. Direction, Inclination, and Intended Approach
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A mover turns toward a chosen target or deviates from the proper line.
- Active motifs: intending and approaching a direction `س و ي:B004/m01`; directing oneself toward another `س و ي:B008/m01`; bodily or facial inclination `ص و ر:B001/m01`; deviation from a route `ع د ل:B006/m01`; deliberate aim toward a thing `ق د م:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: س و ي and ع د ل at 82:7; ص و ر at 82:8; ق د م at 82:5.
- Synthesis: Intention selects the goal, orientation aligns the mover, inclination changes bearing, and deviation marks failure to remain on line. The motifs form a full directional process.

#### Subchannel B. Clear Route, Middle, and Local Position
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A traveler locates a traversable course by its clear trace and central position.
- Active motifs: clear unbroken road `ح ف ظ:B007/m01`; known middle or fair route `س و ي:B006/m02`; center or main line of road and valley `م ل ك:B006/m01`; named locality `ق د م:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ح ف ظ at 82:10; س و ي at 82:7; م ل ك at 82:19; ق د م at 82:5.
- Synthesis: The route supplies continuity, the middle supplies orientation within it, and the locality fixes position. This is navigation by stable spatial structure.

#### Subchannel C. Riding, Sea Travel, and Walking
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Travelers move across land or water by mount, vessel, or foot.
- Active motifs: riding a mount or vessel `ر ك ب:B001/m01`; embarking by sea `ب ح ر:B009/m01`; travel on foot `ن ع م:B012/m01`; foot as the organ of tread `ق د م:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ك ب at 82:8; ب ح ر at 82:3; ن ع م at 82:13; ق د م at 82:5.
- Synthesis: Mount and vessel provide transport, the foot provides unassisted movement, and sea travel supplies environment. The channel keeps distinct travel modes within one journey frame.

#### Subchannel D. Hunt, Ambush, and Targeted Pursuit
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Hunters leave settlement, select prey, conceal themselves, and deploy a trap or shot.
- Active motifs: going out to hunt `س م و:B006/m01`; aiming at a person or place `د ر ي:B002/m01`; stalking under concealment `د ر ي:B003/m01`; hunting trap or snare `ص ل ي:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: س م و at 82:1; د ر ي at 82:17/18; ص ل ي at 82:15.
- Synthesis: Departure begins the hunt, intention selects prey, concealment enables approach, and the trap completes capture. The sequence is a formed pursuit scene rather than a loose animal association.

#### Subchannel E. Chance Encounter, Capture, and Attachment
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An unplanned meeting turns into seizure, attachment, or companionship.
- Active motifs: encountering someone without prior intent `ب ح ر:B010/m01`; taking hold and remaining attached `ب ل ل:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ح ر at 82:3; ب ل ل at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Chance supplies the meeting and capture supplies its possible physical consequence. The scene traces a movement from accidental contact to attachment.

#### Subchannel F. Forepart, Hindpart, and Human-Facing Side
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An object or body is oriented by front, rear, and the side turned toward its user.
- Active motifs: forepart or vanguard `ق د م:B007/m02`; rear or hind section `ء خ ر:B003/m01`; human-facing side of an object or mount `ء ن س:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ق د م and ء خ ر at 82:5; ء ن س at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Front and rear define an axis, while the human-facing side situates a participant relative to it. The channel supplies concrete spatial anatomy to the surah's before-and-after language.

#### Subchannel G. Undertaking, Load, and Riding Pad
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A rider assumes an undertaking and stabilizes the carried load on a mount.
- Active motifs: taking an affair or burden upon oneself `ر ك ب:B003/m01`; riding pad placed over a camel's back `س و ي:B010/m01`; counterbalanced load `ع د ل:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ك ب at 82:8; س و ي and ع د ل at 82:7.
- Synthesis: The undertaking is the figurative burden, while pad and counterweight are its transport mechanics. The scene bridges responsibility and physically carried load.

### 13. Competition, Precedence, and Courage
- Semantic invariant: Participants compare rank through racing, rivalry, boasting, daring, or decisive advance.
- Surface relation: indirect; advancement at 82:5, moral rank at 82:13-14, and the follower relation latent in 82:15 support the channel.
- Surprising reach: Competition joins race order, social boasting, precious stakes, contentious character, battle advance, and an animal that runs then stops.

#### Subchannel A. Race, Second Place, and Interrupted Run
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Competitors move in sequence, with a follower tracking the leader until motion falters.
- Active motifs: second runner following the winner `ص ل ي:B007/m01`; bold forward advance `ق د م:B006/m01`; wild animal running then stopping to look back `ك ذ ب:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ص ل ي at 82:15; ق د م at 82:5; ك ذ ب at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Advance supplies effort, the second-place runner supplies rank, and the interrupted run supplies a failure of continuation. The scene distinguishes movement from competitive placement.

#### Subchannel B. Rivalry, Boasting, and Social Victory
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Rivals publicly compare excellence and seek to surpass one another.
- Active motifs: mutual rivalry and boasting `س م و:B007/m01`; contest in generosity `ك ر م:B006/m01`; overcoming and precedence `ب ر ر:B008/m01`; severe contentiousness `ب ل ل:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: س م و at 82:1; ك ر م at 82:6/11; ب ر ر at 82:13; ب ل ل at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Rivalry establishes comparison, boasting publicizes it, contentiousness intensifies it, and victory supplies the outcome. The channel covers social competition beyond literal racing.

#### Subchannel C. Precious Stakes and Competitive Desire
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A valued object becomes the focus of desire, guarded status, and competition.
- Active motifs: precious object over which people compete `ن ف س:B010/m01`; elite or finest member of a group `غ ر ر:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ف س at 82:5/19; غ ر ر at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Value creates desire and elite standing concentrates it. The scene shows why prestige and rivalry cluster around scarce or honored objects.

#### Subchannel D. Courageous Charge and Violent Contest
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A fighter commits to an advance that may culminate in victory, wounding, or sacrilegious battle.
- Active motifs: battles associated with violated sanctity `ف ج ر:B006/m02`; combative contentiousness `ب ل ل:B005/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ل ل at 82:9; ف ج ر at 82:3/14.
- Synthesis: Contentiousness supplies force and the named battles supply the conflict setting. Courage here is tested by participation in violent contest rather than claimed status.

### 14. Craft, Materials, and Tools
- Semantic invariant: Raw material is measured, joined, sharpened, fired, ground, contained, decorated, or worked by a skilled hand.
- Surface relation: indirect; making at 82:7-8, writing at 82:11, action at 82:12, and fire at 82:15 provide lexical anchors.
- Surprising reach: The craft field includes stitched leather, spear assembly, iron sparkle, adzes, grinding stones, vessel lids, perfume, necklaces, and premature dough.

#### Subchannel A. Leather Joining and Measured Tanning
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Leather is treated in measured doses and stitched into a joined container or surface.
- Active motifs: leather or waterskin stitching `ك ت ب:B001/m02`; small measured tanning dose `ن ف س:B007/m01`; material worker or artisan `ف ع ل:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك ت ب at 82:11; ن ف س at 82:5/19; ف ع ل at 82:12.
- Synthesis: The dose conditions the hide, the artisan performs the work, and stitching turns separate edges into one object. The scene is a compact material-production sequence.

#### Subchannel B. Edge, Horn, Spearhead, and Adze
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hard implement receives a pointed or cutting end suited to combing, piercing, or carving.
- Active motifs: cutting edge of blade or sword `غ ر ر:B012/m01`; pointed comb-like implement `د ر ي:B004/m01`; adze used to carve wood `ق د م:B008/m01`; fitting a spear shaft with a point `ء م ر:B011/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ر ر at 82:6; د ر ي at 82:17/18; ق د م at 82:5; ء م ر at 82:19.
- Synthesis: Hard material supplies the component, sharpening supplies form, and fitting supplies assembly. The tool's function follows from the relation between shaft, point, and worked surface.

#### Subchannel C. Fitted Components and Structural Cohesion
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Separate parts are fixed into a stable whole whose strength comes from tight joining.
- Active motifs: fitted inset or blade component `ر ك ب:B004/m02`; material cohesion and firmness `م ل ك:B001/m01`; sparkle of worked iron `ك و ك ب:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ك ب at 82:8; م ل ك at 82:19; ك و ك ب at 82:2.
- Synthesis: Fitting creates the joint, cohesion makes it load-bearing, and iron brilliance identifies a finished hard material. The channel is assembly viewed at the component level.

#### Subchannel D. Heating, Straightening, and Grinding
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Heat or abrasion changes the shape and texture of a worked object.
- Active motifs: fire used to roast or straighten `ص ل ي:B004/m01`; broad grinding stone `ص ل ي:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ص ل ي at 82:15.
- Synthesis: Heat straightens and the stone crushes. These are distinct operations within one transformative craft field.

#### Subchannel E. Vessel, Lid, Bag, and Thick Paste
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A container is made useful by enclosure, covering, and a substance that coats or seals it.
- Active motifs: carried sack or bag `غ ر ر:B013/m01`; lid set over a jar or pot `ك ر م:B005/m01`; thick syrup or paste used in treatment `ر ب ب:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ر ر and ر ب ب at 82:6; ك ر م at 82:6/11.
- Synthesis: The bag supplies portable containment, the lid closes an opening, and the paste conditions or seals the material. The scene unifies container components and maintenance.

#### Subchannel F. Necklace and Anointment
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A body or object is adorned through a strung ornament or scented coating.
- Active motifs: necklace or strung collar `ك ر م:B003/m01`; scented anointment or colored perfume `خ ل ق:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك ر م at 82:6/11; خ ل ق at 82:7.
- Synthesis: Stringing produces the ornament and anointment changes surface scent or color. Both are deliberate additions to appearance rather than changes to underlying form.

### 15. Body, Physiology, and Health
- Semantic invariant: Life is maintained and perceived through breath, blood, sensory organs, reproduction, nourishment, and bodily integrity.
- Surface relation: indirect; the human body is formed at 82:6-8, the self appears at 82:5 and 82:19, and burning at 82:14-15 supplies health and injury contrasts.
- Surprising reach: The body ledger reaches nostrils, pupils, blood depth, pelvic anatomy, lactation, wasting disease, crisis heat, paunch, scalp itch, and the birth caul.

#### Subchannel A. Breath, Nostril, and Mouth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Air leaves the body through the mouth and nostrils.
- Active motifs: breath leaving the interior `ن ف س:B001/m01`; nostril and expelled contents `ن ث ر:B002/m01`; corners of the mouth `ص و ر:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ف س at 82:5/19; ن ث ر at 82:2; ص و ر at 82:8.
- Synthesis: Breath supplies airflow while nostril and mouth supply its bodily passages. The scene isolates respiration from the later discourse channel built on articulation.

#### Subchannel B. Eye, Pupil, Gaze, and Envy
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: The eye receives an image and directs a charged gaze toward another.
- Active motifs: reflected human figure in the pupil `ء ن س:B005/m01`; inflamed or protruding eye `ج ح م:B003/m01`; envious or harmful eye `ن ف س:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ء ن س at 82:6; ج ح م at 82:14; ن ف س at 82:5/19.
- Synthesis: The pupil supplies optical reception, the protruding eye supplies visible intensity, and the harmful gaze supplies social effect. The scene spans anatomy, perception, and interpersonal force.

#### Subchannel C. Blood and Bodily Depth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Blood occupies bodily depth and functions as a visible life substance.
- Active motifs: flowing blood as life substance `ن ف س:B004/m01`; depth and intense redness of blood `ب ح ر:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ف س at 82:5/19; ب ح ر at 82:3.
- Synthesis: Depth locates the bodily interior and blood supplies its vital material. Intense redness makes the hidden life substance visibly legible when exposed.

#### Subchannel D. Knee, Pubis, Loins, and Hip Socket
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Lower-body joints and reproductive regions support movement, posture, and generation.
- Active motifs: knee and its bone `ر ك ب:B005/m01`; pubic or genital region `ر ك ب:B006/m01`; loins around the tailbone `ص ل ي:B006/m01`; rounded head of the thigh in the hip socket `ك ر م:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ك ب and ص و ر at 82:8; ص ل ي at 82:15; ك ر م at 82:6/11.
- Synthesis: Knee supplies locomotor articulation, the hip supplies a rotating joint, and pubis plus loins locate reproductive anatomy. The motifs form a coherent lower-body map.

#### Subchannel E. Childbirth, Lactation, and Milk Failure
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Birth is followed by maternal recovery and milk provision, which may be scant or cease.
- Active motifs: childbirth and postpartum bleeding `ن ف س:B005/m01`; prolific childbearing `ن ث ر:B006/m01`; recently delivered milk ewe `ر ب ب:B009/m01`; fingertip milking `ف ط ر:B005/m01`; scant milk supply `غ ر ر:B010/m01`; milk that unexpectedly ceases `ك ذ ب:B006/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ف س at 82:5/19; ن ث ر at 82:2; ر ب ب and غ ر ر at 82:6; ف ط ر at 82:1; ك ذ ب at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Birth establishes the maternal state, milking supplies the extraction action, and scantness or cessation supplies failure modes. The scene is a complete reproductive and provisioning process.

#### Subchannel F. Wasting Disease and Convalescence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Illness wastes the body before recovery restores strength.
- Active motifs: wasting disease with loss of flesh `ب ح ر:B011/m01`; back disease in sheep `ر ك ب:B010/m01`; recovery after sickness `ب ل ل:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ح ر at 82:3; ر ك ب at 82:8; ب ل ل at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Wasting and back disease supply distinct pathological states, while convalescence supplies reversal. The channel preserves health as a transition rather than a static condition.

#### Subchannel G. Acute Crisis and Scorching Heat
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An acute illness reaches a decisive turning point under severe heat.
- Active motifs: sudden medical crisis and crisis day `ب ح ر:B012/m02`; scorching July heat `ب ح ر:B012/m03`; severe bodily heat `ج ح م:B001/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ح ر at 82:3; ج ح م at 82:14.
- Synthesis: Acute change supplies the medical event, the crisis day supplies temporal concentration, and scorching heat supplies environmental severity. The same heat that signifies punishment can therefore describe bodily danger.

#### Subchannel H. Abdomen, Scalp, and Birth Caul
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Bodily surfaces and coverings are marked by enlargement, irritation, or a membrane around birth.
- Active motifs: enlarged abdomen `ب ح ر:B013/m01`; scalp itch and desire for scratching `ص و ر:B008/m01`; caul or omental fat `غ ي ب:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ح ر at 82:3; ص و ر at 82:8; غ ي ب at 82:16.
- Synthesis: The abdomen supplies bulk, the scalp supplies irritated surface, and the caul supplies a bodily covering. These concrete anatomical states resist absorption into a generic body label.

### 16. Fire, Anger, and Violent Conflict
- Semantic invariant: Heat intensifies from physical flame into anger, battle, injury, and moral brazenness.
- Surface relation: direct; the wicked are in blazing fire at 82:14 and enter it at 82:15.
- Surprising reach: Fire becomes a face flushed by rage, guarded pride, battlefield heat, armor, nose-first trauma, and shameless exposure.

#### Subchannel A. Flame and Endured Heat
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A fire blazes intensely and a body enters or endures its heat.
- Active motifs: blazing fire and concentrated heat `ج ح م:B001/m01`; entering or suffering fire `ص ل ي:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ج ح م at 82:14; ص ل ي at 82:15.
- Synthesis: Blaze supplies the setting and entering it supplies the affected participant. This is the direct punitive fire scene of 82:14-15.

#### Subchannel B. Anger, Facial Heat, and Guarded Pride
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Inner anger rises as heat, becomes visible in the face, and mobilizes defensive pride.
- Active motifs: face inflamed by anger `ج ح م:B004/m01`; protective anger and pride `ح ف ظ:B005/m01`; nose-tip posture of an angry person `ق ب ر:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ج ح م at 82:14; ح ف ظ at 82:10; ق ب ر at 82:4.
- Synthesis: Heat supplies the bodily metaphor, facial posture supplies visible expression, and protective pride supplies motive. Anger is rendered as a fire moving from interior state to social display.

#### Subchannel C. Battle, Armor, and Wounding
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Armed combat reaches a heated clash in which armor protects bodies from penetrating blows.
- Active motifs: battle at its fiercest and deadliest `ج ح م:B002/m01`; full-body coat of mail `ن ث ر:B005/m01`; blood-drawing combat thrust `ن ث ر:B003/m01`; committed or failed charge `ك ذ ب:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ج ح م at 82:14; ن ث ر at 82:2; ك ذ ب at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Battle supplies the setting, armor the defense, thrust the injury mechanism, and charge the fighter's commitment. The channel is a complete violent-conflict scene.

#### Subchannel D. Brazenness and Violated Restraint
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Shame and moral restraint are cast off, producing exposed and aggressive conduct.
- Active motifs: deficient modesty or shamelessness `ج ح م:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ج ح م at 82:14.
- Synthesis: The motif forms a coherent character state in which restraint and shame are absent, intensifying the fire root's reach from heat into exposed conduct.

### 17. Plant Life, Food, and Agriculture
- Semantic invariant: Plants emerge, propagate, feed animals or humans, and depend on soil and water.
- Surface relation: indirect; water at 82:3, lordly nurture at 82:6, and ease at 82:13 provide the principal anchors.
- Surprising reach: The channel includes wheat, arak berries, palms, mushrooms, pasture grass, hidden roots, vine fruit, and planted rows.

#### Subchannel A. Grain, Berry, and Vine
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Cultivated or gathered plants yield edible grain and fruit.
- Active motifs: wheat and grain food `ب ر ر:B006/m01`; arak berry `ب ر ر:B007/m01`; grapevine and grapes `ك ر م:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ر ر at 82:13; ك ر م at 82:6/11.
- Synthesis: Grain supplies staple food, berry supplies gathered fruit, and vine supplies cultivated fruit. The scene is a compact edible-plant inventory.

#### Subchannel B. Palm Offshoot and Seedling
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A young plant emerges from a parent trunk or grows as a small palm.
- Active motifs: young palms `ش ي ء:B006/m01`; parallel young-palm branch sense `ش ي ء:B008/m01`; palm offshoot growing above the ground `ر ك ب:B007/m01`; cluster or grove of palms `ص و ر:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ش ي ء and ر ك ب and ص و ر at 82:8/19 and 82:8.
- Synthesis: The young palm supplies the organism, the offshoot supplies propagation, and the grove supplies mature grouping. The duplicate palm evidence is consolidated into one botanical process while preserving both branch containers.

#### Subchannel C. Mushroom Emergence and Foraging
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A fungus appears rapidly from the ground and is gathered as food.
- Active motifs: mushroom or truffle-like growth `ف ط ر:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ف ط ر at 82:1.
- Synthesis: The motif forms a complete singleton scene of sudden edible emergence. Its shape and rapid appearance resonate with opening and origination without being reduced to either.

#### Subchannel D. Pasture, Rain-Fed Growth, and Animal Fodder
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Moist soil supports persistent vegetation that becomes pasture.
- Active motifs: evergreen herb or plant `ر ب ب:B012/m01`; grass eaten by camels `ص ل ي:B010/m01`; fertile soil and productive vegetation `ك ر م:B002/m02`; watered meadow in low ground `ب ح ر:B006/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ب ب at 82:6; ص ل ي at 82:15; ك ر م at 82:6/11; ب ح ر at 82:3.
- Synthesis: Watered ground supplies setting, persistent vegetation supplies growth, and fodder supplies ecological function. The scene links atmospheric benefit to animal subsistence.

#### Subchannel E. Hidden Root and Planted Row
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Roots remain concealed below ground while plants are arranged in visible rows above it.
- Active motifs: buried roots later exposed by digging `غ ي ب:B008/m01`; planted row or raised strip of cultivation `ر ك ب:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ي ب at 82:16; ر ك ب at 82:8.
- Synthesis: Hidden roots supply the below-ground support and the planted row supplies ordered surface growth. The contrast forms a vertical agricultural structure.

### 18. Animal Life, Herds, and Birds
- Semantic invariant: Animals gather by species and role as herd, domestic stock, bird, predator, or named young.
- Surface relation: indirect; stars at 82:2, the human at 82:6, and ease at 82:13 activate numerous animal analogies and lexical species.
- Surprising reach: The inventory reaches wild cattle, livestock wealth, a lamb, lark, raptor, nightingale, ostrich, hyena, and animal leadership.

#### Subchannel A. Herds, Cattle, and Livestock Wealth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Domestic or wild animals move as a collective herd and function as subsistence wealth.
- Active motifs: herd of wild cattle or camels `ر ب ب:B014/m01`; cattle herd `ص و ر:B006/m01`; livestock wealth, especially camels `ن ع م:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ب ب at 82:6; ص و ر at 82:8; ن ع م at 82:13.
- Synthesis: Herd supplies collective movement, cattle supplies species form, and livestock supplies economic role. The channel separates animal aggregation from the authority of the leading animal.

#### Subchannel B. Ewe, Lamb, and Recent Birth
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A newly delivered ewe remains near shelter with her young lamb.
- Active motifs: young lamb `ء م ر:B009/m01`; ewe recently delivered and retained for milk `ر ب ب:B009/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ء م ر at 82:19; ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: The lamb supplies offspring and the recently delivered ewe supplies maternal role and setting. This is the animal counterpart to the human birth-and-lactation scene.

#### Subchannel C. Lark, Raptor, Ostrich, and Calling Bird
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Distinct birds are recognized by species, flight, predation, or characteristic call.
- Active motifs: lark `ق ب ر:B003/m01`; falcon or hawk `ع ل م:B006/m01`; ostrich `ن ع م:B006/m01`; nightingale or calling bird `ب ل ل:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ق ب ر at 82:4; ع ل م at 82:5/12; ن ع م at 82:13; ب ل ل at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Species names provide the inventory, predation distinguishes the raptor, and vocalization distinguishes the calling bird. The channel preserves concrete ornithology rather than treating all birds as decorative imagery.

#### Subchannel D. Hyena and Other Wild Species
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Wild animals are identified by sex, species, and relation to open or concealed habitat.
- Active motifs: male hyena `ع ل م:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ع ل م at 82:5/12.
- Synthesis: The branch forms a coherent species-level singleton within the animal parent, keeping a named wild predator visible alongside domesticated herds and birds.

### 19. Speech, Naming, and Discourse
- Semantic invariant: Meaning is produced and managed through articulation, writing, naming, response, correction, lament, and directive speech.
- Surface relation: direct; denial at 82:9, writing at 82:11, knowing acts at 82:12, and repeated questioning at 82:17-18 establish the discourse frame.
- Surprising reach: Speech reaches tongue placement, animal-like calls, grammatical objects, reciprocal action, interjections, naming, and exhortation.

#### Subchannel A. Articulation, Voice, and Noise
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Breath is shaped by tongue placement into speech, call, or noisy vocalization.
- Active motifs: tongue placed at articulation points `ب ل ل:B006/m01`; noisy or repeated speech `ب ر ر:B004/m01`; moan, water-sound, or bird call `ب ل ل:B010/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ل ل at 82:9; ب ر ر at 82:13.
- Synthesis: Tongue placement supplies the mechanism, voice supplies the output, and noise or call supplies variation in communicative form. The channel links bodily phonetics to audible discourse.

#### Subchannel B. Grammar and Syntactic Roles
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Grammatical relations organize participants and modify scope or quantity.
- Active motifs: grammatical objects and complements `ف ع ل:B007/m01`; particle of approximation or quantification `ر ب ب:B015/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ف ع ل at 82:12; ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Grammatical objects assign semantic roles, while the particle modifies relation or quantity. The scene is language treated as structured representation.

#### Subchannel C. Assent and Corrective Redirection
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A speaker first confirms or then redirects the proposition under discussion.
- Active motifs: affirmative yes `ن ع م:B004/m01`; corrective particle bal `ب ل ل:B008/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ع م at 82:13; ب ل ل at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Assent preserves the prior proposition, while correction interrupts and replaces its direction. Their contrast forms a compact dialogue mechanism.

#### Subchannel D. Lament and Emotional Exclamation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Shock or grief interrupts ordinary discourse with an exclamation.
- Active motifs: lamenting exclamation `ش ي ء:B007/m01`; parallel exclamation of grief or amazement `ش ي ء:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ش ي ء at 82:8/19.
- Synthesis: Both branch containers preserve the same discourse event and are grouped under one motif class: affect forces itself into speech as an interruption.

#### Subchannel E. Naming and Invocation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A referent is assigned a name and may then be called upon through that designation.
- Active motifs: naming, name, and namesake `س م و:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: س م و at 82:1.
- Synthesis: Naming establishes a sign-referent relation and makes a person or thing available for classification and direct address.

#### Subchannel F. Exhortation and Binding Speech
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A formula urges a hearer toward an action and presents it as obligatory.
- Active motifs: formula meaning hold to or undertake an act `ك ذ ب:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ك ذ ب at 82:9.
