- Synthesis: The exhortative formula shows speech acting directly upon conduct by presenting an undertaking as immediately incumbent.

#### Subchannel G. Reciprocal Action
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Two participants perform an action in relation to one another.
- Active motifs: action occurring between two parties `ف ع ل:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ف ع ل at 82:12.
- Synthesis: The branch forms a standalone discourse-relation scene within the language parent: the grammatical form encodes mutual participation rather than a one-directional act.

### 20. Self, Character, and Social Relation
- Semantic invariant: A person persists as a living self with identity, inward disposition, intimate bonds, and a position within a community.
- Surface relation: direct; the self appears at 82:5 and twice at 82:19, while the human is addressed at 82:6.
- Surprising reach: Selfhood extends into intellect, courage, confidants, favorite persons, backbiting, hidden reputation, and large human collectives.

#### Subchannel A. Living Self and Personal Identity
- Reading type: surface-primary
- Scene or process: A living human acts and remains the same identifiable subject across judgment.
- Active motifs: life-bearing soul `ن ف س:B011/m01`; identity or very self of a thing `ن ف س:B012/m01`; individual human being `ء ن س:B001/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ف س at 82:5/19; ء ن س at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Life supplies animation, identity supplies persistence, and the human supplies embodied subjecthood. This is the agent who knows deeds at 82:5 and lacks power at 82:19.

#### Subchannel B. Mind, Intention, and Disposition
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Inward cognition directs action while durable character shapes how the person responds.
- Active motifs: intention, intellect, and inward thought `ن ف س:B013/m01`; courage, pride, generosity, or temperament `ن ف س:B014/m01`; ingrained disposition `خ ل ق:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ف س at 82:5/19; خ ل ق at 82:7.
- Synthesis: Intention supplies immediate direction, intellect supplies reflection, and disposition supplies durable tendency. The channel distinguishes momentary thought from formed character.

#### Subchannel C. Intimacy, Favorite, and Admiration
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Familiarity develops into close companionship, trusted confidence, or delighted attachment.
- Active motifs: intimate companionship `ء ن س:B003/m01`; confidant or chosen companion `ء ن س:B006/m01`; admiration and delight in another `ش ي ء:B004/m01`; dearly valued person `ك ر م:B010/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ء ن س and ك ر م at 82:6/11; ش ي ء at 82:8/19.
- Synthesis: Familiarity removes distance, confidence establishes trust, admiration supplies affect, and dearness stabilizes attachment. The scene is a graded movement toward intimacy.

#### Subchannel D. Backbiting and Protection of the Absent
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: An absent person's reputation becomes vulnerable to hostile mention or active defense.
- Active motifs: speaking of a person in absence `غ ي ب:B004/m01`; defending another's absent honor `ح ف ظ:B006/m03`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ي ب at 82:16; ح ف ظ at 82:10.
- Synthesis: Absence creates the vulnerable setting, mention supplies the harmful act, and protection supplies its ethical opposite. The channel turns hiddenness into a social-reputation relation.

#### Subchannel E. Humanity and Multitude
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Individual humans gather into a large social body.
- Active motifs: humankind as a collective `ء ن س:B001/m02`; large assembled groups or thousands `ر ب ب:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ء ن س and ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: The human category supplies membership and the multitude supplies scale. This scene expands the singular addressee of 82:6 into society.

### 21. Household, Kinship, and Lineage
- Semantic invariant: Persons are joined through marriage, descent, care, and obligations toward relatives.
- Surface relation: indirect; lordly nurture at 82:6, formed bodies at 82:7-8, beneficence at 82:13, and possession at 82:19 provide anchors.
- Surprising reach: Household relation includes stepchildren, caregivers, marriage contracts, lineage, favored kin, and care opposed to estrangement.

#### Subchannel A. Marriage, Stepchild, and Caregiver
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Marriage forms a household in which an adult assumes care for a child from an earlier union.
- Active motifs: marriage contract `م ل ك:B004/m01`; stepchild and responsible caregiver `ر ب ب:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: م ل ك at 82:19; ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: Marriage establishes the household relation, the stepchild supplies the dependent participant, and the caregiver supplies the nurturing role. The scene is complete without importing unrelated ownership senses.

#### Subchannel B. Kin Care and Connection
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Relatives maintain connection through kindness, benefit, and protection against estrangement.
- Active motifs: kindness toward parents, children, and kin `ب ر ر:B003/m01`; benefit figured as moistening kinship `ب ل ل:B002/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ر ر at 82:13; ب ل ل at 82:9.
- Synthesis: Kinship establishes obligation, kindness supplies action, and benefit supplies material effect. The moistening image presents care as what keeps a relation from drying out or breaking.

#### Subchannel C. Lineage and Beloved Stock
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person is valued through origin, descent, or membership in an honored line.
- Active motifs: origin, lineage, or noble stock `ر ك ب:B008/m01`; beloved or precious member of one's people `ك ر م:B010/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ك ب at 82:8; ك ر م at 82:6/11.
- Synthesis: Lineage supplies inherited placement and beloved status supplies affective valuation. The channel separates family standing from competitive prestige.

#### Subchannel D. Reproductive Closure
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A bodily blockage prevents ordinary reproductive passage and threatens household continuity.
- Active motifs: imperforate reproductive condition `خ ل ق:B012/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: خ ل ق at 82:7.
- Synthesis: The motif forms a coherent singleton social consequence within the household parent: a concrete bodily closure becomes a barrier to reproduction.

### 22. Place, Settlement, and Habitation
- Semantic invariant: Human and animal activity is situated in settlements, open plains, hollows, forests, and named localities.
- Surface relation: indirect; sea at 82:3, lordship at 82:6, balance at 82:7, absence at 82:16, and dominion at 82:19 supply spatial anchors.
- Surprising reach: Place is defined by obedience, comfort, exposure, concealment, and suitability for residence.

#### Subchannel A. City and Agreeable Residence
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A population settles in a city whose order and environment make residence possible.
- Active motifs: city as settled place of obedience `د ي ن:B006/m02`; land that agrees with its inhabitant `ن ع م:B011/m01`; staying permanently in a place `ر ب ب:B007/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: د ي ن at 82:9/15/17/18; ن ع م at 82:13; ر ب ب at 82:6.
- Synthesis: City supplies built settlement, suitability supplies comfort, and residence supplies continuity. The channel distinguishes habitation from political authority while retaining their connection.

#### Subchannel B. Exposed Plain and Dry Land
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A traveler stands in a broad open tract without concealment or water cover.
- Active motifs: exposed open space `ب ح ر:B005/m01`; dry land and desert opposed to sea `ب ر ر:B005/m01`; wide smooth plain `س و ي:B009/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ب ح ر at 82:3; ب ر ر at 82:13; س و ي at 82:7.
- Synthesis: Exposure supplies visibility, dry land supplies environmental contrast, and the smooth plain supplies traversable form. The place scene is the terrestrial counterpart to broad water.

#### Subchannel C. Hollow, Forest, and Concealed Habitat
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A low place or dense growth hides what enters it.
- Active motifs: sunken hollow or depression `غ ي ب:B002/m02`; forest or thicket used for concealment `غ ي ب:B003/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: غ ي ب at 82:16.
- Synthesis: Depression conceals by depth and forest conceals by density. Their shared invariant is a habitat that removes occupants from ordinary sight.

### 23. Circumstance, Otherness, and Capacity
- Semantic invariant: An entity is described by its current condition, difference from another, available scope, or fitness for action.
- Surface relation: indirect; otherness and sequence at 82:5, formation at 82:7, and the inability of one self to act for another at 82:19 provide anchors.
- Surprising reach: Abstract condition reaches bulk, breadth, temporal allowance, suitability, head-equivalent value, and the distinction between the same and the other.

#### Subchannel A. Affair, Condition, and Dominant Bulk
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A matter is identified by its prevailing state or largest component.
- Active motifs: affair, case, or condition `ء م ر:B001/m01`; customary condition or state `د ي ن:B005/m02`; main bulk or greatest part `ك و ك ب:B005/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ء م ر at 82:19; د ي ن at 82:9/15/17/18; ك و ك ب at 82:2.
- Synthesis: Affair supplies the subject under description, condition supplies its state, and bulk identifies what dominates it. The channel organizes circumstance without treating it as a moral or temporal judgment.

#### Subchannel B. Otherness and Nonidentity
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: One entity is distinguished from another rather than merely placed later in sequence.
- Active motifs: another or non-first entity `ء خ ر:B001/m02`; other-than relation `س و ي:B007/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ء خ ر at 82:5; س و ي at 82:7.
- Synthesis: Both motifs mark nonidentity, but one arises from contrast with the first and the other from explicit exclusion. Their relation clarifies the bridge between temporal laterness and logical otherness.

#### Subchannel C. Breadth, Respite, and Fitness
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A person has room, time, or aptitude sufficient to undertake an action.
- Active motifs: personal scope, distance, or temporal allowance `ن ف س:B015/m01`; fitness or readiness for a role `خ ل ق:B005/m01`; breadth and amplitude `ب ح ر:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ف س at 82:5/19; خ ل ق at 82:7; ب ح ر at 82:3.
- Synthesis: Breadth supplies available extent, respite supplies usable time, and fitness supplies agent capacity. Together they define possibility as a relation between actor and circumstance.

#### Subchannel D. Equivalent Magnitude and Head-Measure
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Value or quantity is described by comparison with a salient bodily measure.
- Active motifs: wealth or favor equivalent to the head `س و ي:B013/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: س و ي at 82:7.
- Synthesis: The head supplies a concrete comparison unit for an equivalent amount of wealth or favor inside an otherwise abstract value field.

### 24. Relief, Recovery, and Release
- Semantic invariant: Constraint is loosened through expanded space, restored health, ransom, or formal emancipation.
- Surface relation: indirect; the self at 82:5 and 82:19, writing at 82:11, and bliss at 82:13 anchor the movement from constraint to release.
- Surprising reach: Relief appears as breathing room, convalescence, substituted payment, transfer out of ownership, and contracted manumission.

#### Subchannel A. Relief from Distress and Restored Ease
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Pressure is relieved and the affected person returns toward ease or health.
- Active motifs: opening relief from distress `ن ف س:B002/m01`; softness, comfort, and ease `ن ع م:B002/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ن ف س at 82:5/19; ن ع م at 82:13.
- Synthesis: Relief removes pressure and comfort supplies the new condition. The process moves from constriction to restored capacity.

#### Subchannel B. Ransom and Formal Emancipation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A bound person is released through an equivalent payment or a staged legal contract.
- Active motifs: ransom or substitute value `ع د ل:B003/m02`; owned person transferred out of possession `م ل ك:B002/m02`; installment manumission contract `ك ت ب:B005/m02`.
- Ayah anchors: ع د ل at 82:7; م ل ك at 82:19; ك ت ب at 82:11.
- Synthesis: Ownership supplies the initial constraint, ransom supplies equivalent substitution, and the written contract supplies the legal mechanism of release. The channel is a coherent transition in status.

## Standalone Subchannels

### S1. Gaming Lots and Their Container
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Marked arrows are gathered in a container and one lot determines a participant's share.
- Active motifs: leather container and collected gaming arrows `ر ب ب:B010/m01`; fifth gaming lot `ن ف س:B016/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: ر ب ب at 82:6; ن ف س at 82:5/19.
- Synthesis: The container supplies the gaming equipment, the marked lot supplies the randomizing token, and the result assigns a personal share. The scene is complete but does not share a strong invariant with the surrounding judgment or uncertainty channels.

### S2. Horn, Comb, and Clarion
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A pointed horn can function as a hard implement, while a hollow horn becomes an instrument sounded by blowing.
- Active motifs: sharpened horn or comb `د ر ي:B004/m02`; horn used for the blast `ص و ر:B004/m01`.
- Ayah anchors: د ر ي at 82:17/18; ص و ر at 82:8.
- Synthesis: Material shape links the pointed implement to the blown horn, but their operations remain distinct. The compact object-centered scene is stronger as a standalone than as a forced member of either craft or speech.
